## Summary

These contracts accept signatures from a key that a function can replace. 1,319 code families, 66,317 addresses, $3.8M held, 31,350 recoveries in the study's two-week window. Rows by rating: LOW 1,319. 165 rows were checked on a real transaction; the others were read from source, judged from bytecode or placed by measured rules (each row's note says which).

## Mechanism

PQ-17. Once that key's account has real code its signatures stop counting; its holder rotates first, or the owner afterwards.

## Evidence

- Each row's note says how it was decided: read from verified source, judged from bytecode and function names, placed by a rule measured against hand readings, or executed: a real transaction replayed on the fork's reference implementation with only the precompile changed, state at block 26,438,999.

## Impact and exposure

Largest rows by value held at block 26,438,999:
- `0x3f98beb42c618d31c4f60008de15401bdc84f83f` (unverified): $1.2M, LOW, 3 addresses
- `0x3cda13cdab0c80339980721822689d998eca761a` (deployed by Apyx: Deployer 3): $554k, LOW, 2 addresses

- `0x45804880de22913dafe09f4980848ece6ecbaf78` (PAXG): $115k, LOW, 2 addresses

- `0x223ca78df868367d214b444d561b9123c018963a` (unverified): $43k, LOW

Every row is in the header; `generated/triage_contracts.csv` lists them for search.

## Remediation

Rotate the key before its account takes code.
